Each pump is the spec pump (SPECK TOEMA065200) hydraulics at half the flow: two pumps in parallel deliver the spec's one-pump curve (Fig 5.1.2), three exceed the spec's one-pump curve. Each pump has its own check valve on the discharge, its own VFD (ABB ACH580 class) and its own mains feed.\n\n| Item | Value |\n| --- | --- |\n| Pumps | 3, in parallel, common suction (expansion tank 15 psig) and common discharge header |\n| Rated point per pump | 250 GPM at ~104 psi pump head, 100 % speed (curve fit; the spec pump nameplate says 500 GPM at 112 psi) |\n| Pump head at 100 % speed, per pump | H(q) = 120 + 0.0744 q - 5.524e-4 q^2 psi (q = GPM through that pump) |\n| Affinity | at speed n (fraction): H(q, n) = n^2 H(q/n) |\n| Common internal loss (HX secondary + filter + piping) | 5.89e-5 Q^2 psi, Q = CDU flow (8.7 psi of it is the HX, Fig 5.3.1) |\n| Available dP at the CDU flanges | pump head - internal loss: two pumps at 100 %: 120 + 0.0372 Q - 1.97e-4 Q^2 (spec Fig 5.1.2) |\n| Brake power per pump, 100 % speed | 4.5 + 0.044 q kW (15.5 kW at 250 GPM); affinity: P(q, n) = n^3 P(q/n) |\n| Motor | 18.5 kW per pump; motor + VFD efficiency ~0.93 |\n| VFD | speed command 0-100 %; below 20 % the drive stops the pump; accel/decel ramp 10 %/s; on a drive fault or loss of mains the pump coasts down (~10 s) |\n| Check valves | one per pump, swing type, crack 0.5 psi |\n\nParallel operation note (modeled hydraulic behavior): pumps with flat curves run in parallel share flow badly when their heads differ; the weaker pump can be pushed back to shut-off behind its check valve and run without flow.\n\n## Spec curves (Deschutes v1.0 figure fits, Q in GPM, dP in psi)\n\nTwo pumps (spec pumps) running, available pressure, PG25 (Fig 5.1.1):\n\n| Speed | Fit |\n| --- | --- |\n| 100 % | 119 + 0.0211 Q - 9.34e-5 Q^2 |\n| 90 % | 94.1 + 0.0148 Q - 8.41e-5 Q^2 |\n| 80 % | 74.4 + 0.0107 Q - 7.96e-5 Q^2 |\n| 66 % | 53.2 + 0.0103 Q - 9.07e-5 Q^2 |\n| 50 % | 29.6 + 0.0112 Q - 9.88e-5 Q^2 |\n\nOne spec pump running (Fig 5.1.2), 100 %: 120 + 0.0372 Q - 1.97e-4 Q^2.\n\nHeat exchanger secondary side dP, 2-pass (Fig 5.3.1): dP = -0.0131 + 1.41e-3 Q + 3.19e-5 Q^2 (8.7 psi at 500 GPM). Actuator Belimo GKRX24-MFT class, 90 s full stroke as set here, position feedback from the actuator (2-10 V). They expose the thermal-liquid plant, instrumentation, fault behavior and scenario inputs. Authoring used MATLAB/Simulink; execution uses Python/FMPy without MATLAB or Modelica. Controller authority is limited to the declared 23 observations and four actuator outputs.\n\n## Plant contents\n\n- TCS (secondary): expansion tank at pump suction (15 psig); 3 centrifugal pumps in parallel (pumps-and-hydraulics.md), each with a check valve; internal loss (filter, meter); Alfa Laval HX hot side (fitted to spec Table 5.3); supply flow meter and P/T; 50 ft supply wall manifold; 4 rack groups (isolation valve, quadratic rack impedance, 100 L, heat input); return manifold; return P/T.\n- FWS (primary): facility supply header at T_FWS, facility differential (test case), strainer/pipe loss, HX cold side (P/T at HX inlet and outlet), primary control valve (equal-%, Cv 290, 90 s stroke), facility return header.\n- VFDs: speed command 0-100 %; a command below 20 % stops the pump; ramp 10 %/s. A drive fault or loss of mains stops the drive (pump coasts down) and raises the drive's fault/offline flag; after mains returns the drive reboots in about 5 s, clears the flag and follows its speed command again (flying restart). A tripped drive (fault) stays off.\n- Pump wear: a worn impeller makes less head at the same speed (the drive still reports the commanded speed); its power changes with both the hydraulic capacity loss and the separate efficiency loss (/app/docs/qualification.md).\n- A pump's check valve can stick open: a stopped pump then passes reverse flow from the discharge header back to suction (the model represents the reverse leakage path, not reverse-shaft dynamics); the path closes once that pump runs.\n- Primary valve: the actuator can stall on a seized stem (position feedback stays at the valve position) or the valve can stick and jump (packing friction).\n- Instruments and their failure behaviour: instruments.md.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Simultaneous extreme corners are not required; combined degradation and pump loss are used only where the remaining equipment can supply the requested flow.\n\n| Item | Qualification envelope | Basis and limitation |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Pump head curve | Per-pump multiplier 0.95–1.05 on the nominal head ordinate | [ours] qualification range informed by ISO 9906:2012 Table 8 grade 2B head tolerance ±5%; this is a model envelope, not a claim the installed SPECK pumps have this acceptance grade |\n| Pump capacity curve | Per-pump multiplier 0.92–1.08 on the flow abscissa | [ours] qualification range informed by ISO 9906 Table 8 grade 2B flow tolerance ±8%; independent pumps need not share exactly at equal VFD speed; ISO acceptance tolerances combine manufacturing and measurement tolerances and do not assert a probability distribution |\n| Pump efficiency | Per-pump relative efficiency 0.95–1.02 | [ours] spread around nominal, informed by ISO 9906 grade 2B efficiency tolerance −5%; the positive side is an author assumption, not an ISO upper acceptance limit |\n| Impeller condition | Equivalent-speed factor 0.90–1.00; additional relative efficiency 0.94–1.00 | [ours] reduced-order wear model: hydraulic head and capacity follow affinity scaling while wear-related leakage can increase the shaft power needed per delivered hydraulic watt. Equipment spread changes the readings and hydraulic behavior used for control. The wider equipment model supports faults on either redundant channel and recurring faults; the complete fault scope is specified in the work request. Calibration spread and legitimate rack service remain healthy conditions.\n\n\n## Implemented model variations\n\nThe pump tables are actually changed: `flow_rate_1D_TLU = nominal * pump_flow`, `head_1D_TLU = nominal * pump_head`, and power is scaled by head × flow / efficiency. The same power scaling is applied to VFD kW telemetry. Wear acts on the hydraulic effective speed and separately on efficiency; it changes the physical pump behavior.\n\nThe facility valve remains a physical valve. Its effective opening is mapped to the required Cv characteristic, while `valve_pos` reports actual stem position. Stiction and seizure act on that stem position, before the characteristic. Bias, gain, seeded noise and slow drift act on instrument signals independently of fault injection. Fault mode 6 is a recurring 10 s invalid burst every 60 s; it recovers between bursts.\n\n\n## Site operating range\n\nThe row has four rack groups, each designed for 125 GPM. The acceptance runs use facility supply water from 17 to 30 °C, room dew point from 12 to 21 °C, load-bank and training workloads up to 2 MW, and partial-row service with deliberate rack-group isolation and restoration. These ranges describe the supplied tests; they do not imply every cross-product corner is required. This release uses water and 25 psid facility differential pressure; PG25 is not part of this 31-case qualification scope.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_52","type":"command_execution","command":"/bin/bash -lc \"sed -n '1,380p' /app/submission/controller.py\"","aggregated_output":"\"\"\"Four actuator CDU controller.\n\nThe secondary pumps are controlled from the measured header differential\npressure.  This naturally reduces total flow when a rack branch is isolated\nwhile preserving the pressure, and therefore flow, through the branches that\nremain in service.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom collections import deque\nimport math\n\n\ndef _clip(value, low, high):\n    return min(high, max(low, value))\n\n\ndef _median(values):\n    if not values:\n        return 0.0\n    ordered = sorted(values)\n    n = len(ordered)\n    mid = n // 2\n    if n & 1:\n        return ordered[mid]\n    return 0.5 * (ordered[mid - 1] + ordered[mid])\n\n\nclass Controller:\n    \"\"\"CDU supply controller with no external dependencies or case inputs.\"\"\"\n\n    def reset(self):\n        self.scan = 0\n\n        self.dp_filtered = None\n        self.dp_previous = None\n        self.pump_command = 100.0\n        self.pump_offsets = [0.0, 0.0, 0.0]\n        self.pump_flow_estimates = [None, None, None]\n\n        self.temp_raw_1 = deque(maxlen=80)\n        self.temp_raw_2 = deque(maxlen=80)\n        self.temp_good_1 = deque(maxlen=80)\n        self.temp_good_2 = deque(maxlen=80)\n        self.tret_history = deque(maxlen=80)\n        self.temp_diff_reference = None\n        self.temp_reference_count = 0\n        self.temp_suspect = None\n        self.temp_suspect_count = 0\n        self.temp_bad = None\n        self.temp_bad_clear = 0\n        self.last_temperature = None\n        self.temperature_rate = 0.0\n\n        self.valve_integral = None\n        self.previous_valve_position = 50.0\n        self.valve_command = 50.0\n\n    def _slope(self, history):\n        \"\"\"Robust temperature slope over a 10 second window, in K/s.\"\"\"\n        values = list(history)\n        if len(values) < 40:\n            return None\n        recent = _median(values[-20:])\n        older = _median(values[-40:-20])\n        return (recent - older) / 10.0\n\n    def _mad(self, history):\n        values = list(history)\n        if len(values) < 10:\n            return 0.0\n        middle = _median(values[-20:])\n        return _median([abs(value - middle) for value in values[-20:]])\n\n    def _temperature(self, observation):\n        raw1 = float(observation['T_sup1'])\n        raw2 = float(observation['T_sup2'])\n        tret = float(observation['T_ret'])\n        valid1 = -5.0 <= raw1 <= 105.0\n        valid2 = -5.0 <= raw2 <= 105.0\n\n        self.temp_raw_1.append(raw1)\n        self.temp_raw_2.append(raw2)\n        self.tret_history.append(tret)\n        if valid1:\n            self.temp_good_1.append(raw1)\n        if valid2:\n            self.temp_good_2.append(raw2)\n\n        f1 = _median(list(self.temp_good_1)[-5:]) if self.temp_good_1 else None\n        f2 = _median(list(self.temp_good_2)[-5:]) if self.temp_good_2 else None\n        s1 = self._slope(self.temp_good_1)\n        s2 = self._slope(self.temp_good_2)\n        sr = self._slope(self.tret_history)\n\n        # A failed channel can be open circuit, frozen, or much noisier than\n        # its peer.  The latter two checks use several seconds of evidence so\n        # normal transmitter noise does not switch channels.\n        frozen1 = False\n        frozen2 = False\n        if len(self.temp_raw_1) >= 12 and valid1:\n            tail = list(self.temp_raw_1)[-12:]\n            frozen1 = max(tail) - min(tail) < 1e-5\n        if len(self.temp_raw_2) >= 12 and valid2:\n            tail = list(self.temp_raw_2)[-12:]\n            frozen2 = max(tail) - min(tail) < 1e-5\n\n        movement1 = (max(list(self.temp_good_1)[-24:]) - min(list(self.temp_good_1)[-24:])\n                     if len(self.temp_good_1) >= 12 else 0.0)\n        movement2 = (max(list(self.temp_good_2)[-24:]) - min(list(self.temp_good_2)[-24:])\n                     if len(self.temp_good_2) >= 12 else 0.0)\n        if frozen1 and (movement2 > 0.12 or abs(tret - list(self.tret_history)[-12]) > 0.2):\n            valid1 = False\n        if frozen2 and (movement1 > 0.12 or abs(tret - list(self.tret_history)[-12]) > 0.2):\n            valid2 = False\n\n        mad1 = self._mad(self.temp_good_1)\n        mad2 = self._mad(self.temp_good_2)\n        if mad1 > 0.45 and mad2 < 0.22:\n            valid1 = False\n        if mad2 > 0.45 and mad1 < 0.22:\n            valid2 = False\n\n        # Establish the normal offset between the two wells during steady\n        # operation.  A slow fault drift then appears as a persistent change\n        # in that offset.  Return temperature provides a third trend reference\n        # to tell which supply channel is moving abnormally.\n        if valid1 and valid2 and f1 is not None and f2 is not None:\n            difference = f1 - f2\n            if (self.temp_diff_reference is None and s1 is not None and s2 is not None\n                    and abs(s1) < 0.02 and abs(s2) < 0.02\n                    and abs(s1 - s2) < 0.004):\n                self.temp_reference_count += 1\n                if self.temp_reference_count >= 20:\n                    self.temp_diff_reference = difference\n\n            candidate = None\n            if (self.temp_diff_reference is not None and s1 is not None and s2 is not None\n                    and sr is not None and abs(difference - self.temp_diff_reference) > 0.60\n                    and abs(s1 - s2) > 0.005\n                    # Do not mistake the faster first thermowell for a fault\n                    # during a real load step.  A transmitter drift remains\n                    # visible after the shared fluid temperature settles.\n                    and max(abs(s1), abs(s2), abs(sr)) < 0.04):\n                score1 = abs(s1 - sr)\n                score2 = abs(s2 - sr)\n                if score1 > score2 + 0.002:\n                    candidate = 1\n                elif score2 > score1 + 0.002:\n                    candidate = 2\n\n            if candidate is not None:\n                if self.temp_suspect == candidate:\n                    self.temp_suspect_count += 1\n                else:\n                    self.temp_suspect = candidate\n                    self.temp_suspect_count = 1\n                if self.temp_suspect_count >= 8:\n                    self.temp_bad = candidate\n                    self.temp_bad_clear = 0\n            else:\n                self.temp_suspect = None\n                self.temp_suspect_count = 0\n\n            if self.temp_bad is not None:\n                baseline_ok = abs(difference - self.temp_diff_reference) < 0.28\n                if baseline_ok and abs(s1 - s2) < 0.004:\n                    self.temp_bad_clear += 1\n                    if self.temp_bad_clear >= 60:\n                        self.temp_bad = None\n                        self.temp_bad_clear = 0\n                else:\n                    self.temp_bad_clear = 0\n\n        if self.temp_bad == 1:\n            temperature = f2 if valid2 else f1\n        elif self.temp_bad == 2:\n            temperature = f1 if valid1 else f2\n        elif valid1 and valid2:\n            temperature = 0.5 * (f1 + f2)\n        elif valid1:\n            temperature = f1\n        elif valid2:\n            temperature = f2\n        else:\n            # The two supply RTDs are not expected to fail together.  Return\n            # temperature still gives a conservative fallback for hot-water\n            # control if that occurs.\n            temperature = min(54.0, max(0.0, tret - 6.0))\n\n        if self.last_temperature is None:\n            rate = 0.0\n        else:\n            instant_rate = (temperature - self.last_temperature) / 0.5\n            self.temperature_rate = 0.65 * self.temperature_rate + 0.35 * instant_rate\n            rate = self.temperature_rate\n        self.last_temperature = temperature\n        return temperature, rate\n\n    def _balance_pumps(self, observation, faults):\n        \"\"\"Nudge unequal parallel pumps toward a more even hydraulic share.\n\n        The VFD kW registers are useful here: a pump with normal shaft speed\n        but very low absorbed power is contributing little flow.  The estimate\n        is intentionally used only as a slow trim; header pressure remains the\n        primary capacity and safety signal.\n        \"\"\"\n        flows = []\n        active_indices = []\n        for index, name in enumerate('ABC'):\n            speed = max(0.0, min(100.0, float(observation['spd' + name]))) / 100.0\n            power = max(0.0, float(observation['kW' + name]))\n            if faults[index] or speed < 0.55 or power < 0.2:\n                self.pump_flow_estimates[index] = None\n                flows.append(None)\n                continue\n            # Nominal input power includes motor/VFD losses.  Reverse-solving\n            # the published affinity-scaled power curve gives a useful\n            # relative, rather than metrology-grade, per-pump flow estimate.\n            shaft_power = power * 0.93\n            estimate = speed * (shaft_power / (speed ** 3) - 4.5) / 0.044\n            estimate = _clip(estimate, 0.0, 500.0)\n            previous = self.pump_flow_estimates[index]\n            if previous is None:\n                filtered = estimate\n            else:\n                filtered = 0.25 * estimate + 0.75 * previous\n            self.pump_flow_estimates[index] = filtered\n            flows.append(filtered)\n            active_indices.append(index)\n\n        if len(active_indices) != 3 or self.scan < 40:\n            return self.pump_offsets\n\n        average = sum(flows[index] for index in active_indices) / 3.0\n        if average < 25.0:\n            return self.pump_offsets\n        threshold = max(20.0, 0.12 * average)\n        for index in active_indices:\n            deviation = average - flows[index]\n            if abs(deviation) > threshold:\n                self.pump_offsets[index] += _clip(0.006 * deviation, -0.4, 0.4)\n            else:\n                self.pump_offsets[index] *= 0.995\n\n        mean_offset = sum(self.pump_offsets[index] for index in active_indices) / 3.0\n        for index in active_indices:\n            self.pump_offsets[index] = _clip(self.pump_offsets[index] - mean_offset, -8.0, 8.0)\n        return self.pump_offsets\n\n    def step(self, observation):\n        self.scan += 1\n\n        # The pressure taps remain useful even when either dP transmitter is\n        # failed.  A median of the three independent readings rejects one bad\n        # dP channel without relying on fault-specific schedules.\n        ps = float(observation['P_sup'])\n        pr = float(observation['P_ret'])\n        dp_candidates = [ps - pr]\n        for key in ('dP1', 'dP2'):\n            value = float(observation[key])\n            if 0.0 <= value <= 150.0:\n                dp_candidates.append(value)\n        dp_now = _median(dp_candidates)\n        if self.dp_filtered is None:\n            self.dp_filtered = dp_now\n            self.dp_previous = dp_now\n        else:\n            # Pressure transmitters are quiet, but the hydraulic model has a\n            # short settling oscillation after every VFD update.  A two second\n            # filter keeps the speed loop from chasing that oscillation.\n            self.dp_filtered = 0.20 * dp_now + 0.80 * self.dp_filtered\n        dp_rate = (self.dp_filtered - self.dp_previous) / 0.5\n        self.dp_previous = self.dp_filtered\n\n        faults = [float(observation[f'fault{name}']) > 0.5 for name in 'ABC']\n        any_fault = any(faults)\n\n        # Hold the highest useful speed reserve during normal operation.  A\n        # single drive loss then leaves two pumps close to their full-flow\n        # point.  The pressure feedback trims speed as rack branches are\n        # isolated; its prediction term acts before the 95 psid limit.\n        emergency = self.dp_filtered > 92.0 or ps > 112.0\n        if any_fault:\n            desired_speed = 100.0\n        else:\n            # The proportional trim deliberately has low gain.  The pump\n            # curve is steep enough that a small speed change moves header\n            # pressure several psi; slower output movement avoids a limit\n            # cycle around the differential-pressure target.\n            dp_target = 84.0\n            desired_speed = 100.0 - 0.80 * max(0.0, self.dp_filtered - dp_target)\n\n        # Independent pressure guards operate even when a drive is offline.\n        desired_speed = min(desired_speed,\n                            100.0 - 0.75 * max(0.0, self.dp_filtered - 89.0),\n                            100.0 - 0.70 * max(0.0, ps - 109.0))\n        desired_speed = _clip(desired_speed, 25.0, 100.0)\n\n        if any_fault:\n            self.pump_command = 100.0\n        elif emergency:\n            # A quick drop is safe because the drive itself still enforces\n            # its 10 %/s ramp.  It prevents a pressure pulse during row\n            # isolation or a VFD restart.\n            self.pump_command = min(self.pump_command, desired_speed)\n        else:\n            # Follow normal trims at a bounded rate so small sensor noise and\n            # pressure ripple cannot make the drives hunt.\n            change = _clip(desired_speed - self.pump_command, -0.75, 0.75)\n            self.pump_command += change\n        pump_speed = _clip(self.pump_command, 25.0, 100.0)\n        if any_fault:\n            self.pump_offsets = [0.0, 0.0, 0.0]\n            self.pump_flow_estimates = [None, None, None]\n            individual_speeds = [pump_speed, pump_speed, pump_speed]\n        else:\n            offsets = self._balance_pumps(observation, faults)\n            individual_speeds = [\n                _clip(pump_speed + offsets[index], 25.0, 100.0)\n                for index in range(3)\n            ]\n\n        temperature, temperature_rate = self._temperature(observation)\n        dew = float(observation['dew'])\n        tfs = float(observation['T_fs'])\n        target = _clip(dew + 5.0, 15.0, 50.0)\n        error = temperature - target\n        predicted_error = error + 16.0 * temperature_rate\n\n        valve_pos = _clip(float(observation['valve_pos']), 0.0, 100.0)\n        if self.valve_integral is None:\n            self.valve_integral = valve_pos\n        kp = 5.0\n        ki = 0.025\n        raw_valve = self.valve_integral + kp * predicted_error\n        valve_command = _clip(raw_valve, 0.0, 100.0)\n\n        # Back-calculation prevents integral windup when the 90 second valve\n        # stroke reaches either end stop.\n        saturated_error = valve_command - raw_valve\n        self.valve_integral += ki * error * 0.5 + saturated_error * (0.5 / 18.0)\n        self.valve_integral = _clip(self.valve_integral, -100.0, 200.0)\n\n        # When facility water is below the condensation-safe temperature,\n        # close the valve before the slow actuator can carry the supply below\n        # its lower safety margin.  Re-open once load pushes the supply warm.\n        cold_facility = tfs < dew + 2.0\n        if cold_facility and temperature < dew + 6.0:\n            valve_command = 0.0\n            self.valve_integral = 0.0\n        elif temperature >= 52.0:\n            valve_command = 100.0\n            self.valve_integral = min(100.0, self.valve_integral)\n\n        valve_command = _clip(valve_command, 0.0, 100.0)\n        self.previous_valve_position = valve_pos\n        self.valve_command = valve_command\n\n        return {\n            'pumpA_cmd': float(individual_speeds[0]),\n            'pumpB_cmd': float(individual_speeds[1]),\n            'pumpC_cmd': float(individual_speeds[2]),\n            'valve_cmd': float(valve_command),\n        }\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
